212–267 \subsection{Fixed-Weight Neural Noise and Phase-Specific Stimuli}\label{sec:app_current_noise}\label{sec:app_biw2l_stimulus} With the stage-2 weights fixed, ind…
212–267 \subsection{Fixed-Weight Neural Noise and Phase-Specific Stimuli}\label{sec:app_current_noise}\label{sec:app_biw2l_stimulus} With the stage-2 weights fixed, independent ongoing current noise was applied to four RG cells. Three seeds were evaluated at each stationary standard deviation of \qtylist{0.01;0.025}{\nano\ampere}, with a \qty{20}{\milli\second} correlation time. All six noisy trials completed and retained the principal-motion, knee, and frontal-separation criteria. A zero-noise control and repeated noisy seed also passed. The repeated seed reproduced joint states, neural voltages, and applied noise exactly in a fresh process. This tests the selected air condition under continuous neural-current disturbance; it is distinct from perturbing fixed connection parameters or testing ground balance. Matched baseline and stimulus runs applied \qty{2}{\nano\ampere}, \qty{100}{\milli\second} pulses separately to the right RG-F, right hip-flexor PF, and right iliacus MN, together with a \qty{-2}{\nano\ampere} right PF control. The silent-phase series begins at \qty{7.10}{\second}, and the active-phase series at \qty{7.65}{\second}. Pre-pulse neural trajectories agree exactly. The silent-phase PF pulse reaches only \qty{-3.058}{\milli\volt}, below the outgoing ramp's \qty{0}{\milli\volt} onset; transmission saturates at \qty{5}{\milli\volt}. Its MN difference is below \qty{6e-8}{\milli\volt}. During the active phase, the excitatory PF pulse changes PF voltage by up to \qty{1.14}{\milli\volt} and right iliacus MN voltage by \qty{0.16}{\milli\volt}. The active-phase direct MN pulse changes its voltage by \qty{1.79}{\milli\volt} and subsequently right RG-F voltage by \qty{6.04}{\milli\volt} through the closed neural--mechanical loop. Each maximum is the absolute paired difference over the interval from onset, inclusive, to \qty{3.4}{\second} after onset, exclusive: \qtyrange{7.10}{10.50}{\second} for the silent series and \qtyrange{7.65}{11.05}{\second} for the active series, with the upper endpoint excluded. These are trajectory differences, not direct synaptic gains or changes restricted to the pulse duration. \begin{figure}[p] \centering \includegraphics[width=14cm]{AIR_stimulus_neural.pdf} \caption[Active-phase interventions in the coupled BiW2Lvar+ model]{Applied current and right-side neural responses to separate RG-F, hip-flexor PF, and iliacus MN pulses in suspended BiW2Lvar+. Pulses act from \qty{7.65}{\second} to \qty{7.75}{\second}, during the active phase; the silent-phase control is described separately in the text. All raw samples in the common \qtyrange{7.50}{11.05}{\second} display window are retained. Each condition uses the same learned parameters and mechanics.} \label{fig:app_biw2l_stimulus} \end{figure} \begin{figure}[p] \ContinuedFloat \centering \includegraphics[width=14cm]{AIR_stimulus_mechanics.pdf} \caption[]{Right hip, knee, and ankle responses in the same active-phase trials, on the common time axis. Knee coordinates retain native negative flexion. Shading marks the \qty{100}{\milli\second} stimulus, while the reported maximum differences use the longer \qty{3.4}{\second} response window.} \end{figure} \FloatBarrier \subsection{A PF-Timing Failure Exposed by Intermediate Verification}\label{sec:app_pf_timing} Earlier feedforward and feedback output fits for the s3k and syn6 families did not establish that their implemented PF layers produced the intended timing. A subsequent audit evaluated actual outgoing transmission, $\operatorname{clip}(V/\qty{5}{\milli\volt},0,1)$, on the original calibration clock without shifting individual channels to improve alignment. Here $V$ is rest-relative membrane voltage. It averaged nine cycles at the \qty{1.233}{\second} calibration period, sampling \qtyrange{2.466}{13.556835}{\second}. Transmission greater than 0.2 defined burst windows. The combined provisional check required absolute fundamental lag no greater than \ang{30}, duty error no greater than 0.15, matching burst counts, and transmission excursion at least 0.2. These are declared engineering acceptance criteria, rather than biological laws. None of the twelve syn6 channels passed that combined check. Actual centered output variance required three components per side for \qty{99}{\percent} coverage, compared with six for its intended primitive targets. The fifth primitive remained active throughout the cycle on both sides, whereas intended duties were approximately 0.325 and 0.330. The corresponding s3k outputs required two variance components per side, but numerical intended onset and duration windows had not been declared. Variance rank describes the measured waveforms; it is neither a neuron count nor a proof of minimal circuit size. Figure~\ref{fig:app_pf_timing} shows why an acceptable downstream activation fit can conceal inadequate intermediate timing. \begin{figure}[p] \centering \includegraphics[width=14cm]{PF_timing_authoritative.pdf} \caption[Implemented syn6 PF transmission versus intended primitives]{Raw right-side syn6 S1, S2, and S5 transmission over two calibration cycles, \qtyrange{2.466}{4.932}{\second}. Solid blue curves show actual transmission; dashed black curves show the intended primitive, and dotted lines mark the 0.2 activity threshold. All stored samples are retained, without channel-specific phase shifts. S1 and S2 have displaced timing, while S5 remains active. The zero-of-twelve verdict comes from the separate full nine-cycle averaged audit. This offline diagnostic is separate from the older-readout syn6 air candidate.} \label{fig:app_pf_timing} \end{figure} A bounded gain-only repair improved the mirrored-target objective and reproduced it in a new process, but still passed zero of twelve channels. It was not accepted or installed in the body adapter. Subsequent experiments changed the timing design rather than treating this failed repair as sufficient. 224–281 \subsection{Fixed-Weight Neural Noise and Phase-Specific Stimuli}\label{sec:app_current_noise}\label{sec:app_biw2l_stimulus} I tested stage 2 under independent ongoing current noise in four RG cells with a \qty{20}{\milli\second} correlation time. 269–320 \subsection{W2L108 Ground Stepping and an Unmet Shuffle Target}\label{sec:app_w2l108_ground} A separate 108-cell, 216-connection Li--Deng-derived W2L controller was calibrated for its twelve-muscle mechanical body. This model's anatomical angles include initial-pose offsets and differ from the Gait2392 flexion-negative coordinates. Upward rail/guide assistance averaged \qty{29.519}{\percent} of the \qty{411.2254}{\newton} model weight over $1\leq t<10$\,s; continuous harness force was zero, but passive rails and slack guides remained. The positive upward measure excludes downward assistance contributions. The \qty{35.069}{\percent} signed net assistance instead retains force signs over $0\leq t<10$\,s. Its different window prevents direct comparison as an upward-force reduction. Its \qty{0.908}{\meter} belt-relative progression is not overground displacement. Peak foot force reached 11.727 body weights, identifying impact transients that still require investigation. A slower candidate completed \qty{16}{\second}, with seven qualified swings per side and \qty{10.105}{\percent} upward assistance over \qtyrange{2}{16}{\second}. It did not meet the requested short-shuffle criteria: fore-aft excursions were \qtylist{0.351;0.417}{\meter}, and sustained loaded double support occupied \qty{1.929}{\percent} of the analysis window, below the declared \qty{8}{\percent} target. 322–347 \subsection{Pathway Deletions and Stimulus Tests}\label{sec:app_w2l_perturbations} The recovered W2L configuration was evaluated with nominal weight support set to 0.25 and the remaining controller settings fixed. The control configuration inherits a \qty{40}{\newton} forward pulse from \qty{8}{\second} to \qty{8.15}{\second} in the retained evaluator. Its explicitly named forward-push recording is identical to the control in every saved numerical array, so it is not an independent push-versus-unforced comparison. Mild and strong drive reductions, reversal to a backward \qty{40}{\newton} pulse, and separate Ia, Ib, Renshaw, crossed-heel, and heel-cascade deletions retained the supported-stepping criterion. Their effects differed: Renshaw deletion reduced forward displacement to \qty{0.081}{\meter}, while commissural deletion failed bilateral stepping. Disabling all sensory feedback retained joint cycling and \qty{1.996}{\meter} progression but shifted median contact phase to 0.223 cycles, outside the accepted alternation range. The intact comparison produced \qty{2.239}{\meter} and a phase of 0.498 cycles. These experiments distinguish rhythm generation, progression, and bilateral contact coordination, rather than treating them as interchangeable outcomes. \subsection{CAD Robot: Contact Handover and Supported Clearance}\label{sec:app_cad_supported_stance} The CAD-derived model retains its four-bar knees and spherical hips, using the anatomical frame $x$ anterior, $y$ superior, and $z$ right. The selected controller has 262 graded cells, 230 inputs, and 54 reference-tension actuators. RG activity supplies timing, while an engineering force allocator supplies posture, stance-load, and clearance targets to the MN layer. MN target-current compensation limits normal PF/reflex authority over those outputs. The model therefore evaluates this hybrid architecture, not a complete unmodified neural controller with calibrated dynamic BPA forces. The selected condition completed \qty{28}{\second}. During $16\leq t<28$\,s, mean vertical harness force was \qty{570.20625}{\newton}, or \qty{77.5}{\percent} of the \qty{735.75}{\newton} weight of the \qty{75}{\kilogram} model. Seven right and six left qualifying unloaded-clear intervals produced twelve alternations. Each interval required whole-foot mesh clearance above \qty{5}{\milli\meter}, ipsilateral normal load below \qty{7.36}{\newton}, contralateral load above \qty{15}{\newton}, and duration of at least \qty{80}{\milli\second}. The retained every-\qty{2}{\milli\second} knee-bound accumulator reports whole-trial minimum flexion of \ang{11.20} on the right and \ang{5.10} on the left, against a \ang{1} guard. The saved \qty{10}{\milli\second} traces are a different sampling interval and do not independently reconstruct every physics-step extremum. The \qty{-74.7}{\milli\meter} world-forward displacement describes guided stepping approximately in place. Orientation and lateral guidance remained active. \begin{figure}[p] \centering \includegraphics[width=14cm]{CAD_supported_traces_20261116.pdf} \caption[Quantitative supported CAD stepping]{CAD signals during $16\leq t<28$\,s: RG flexor-minus-extensor voltage (A), native flexion-negative knees (B), foot normal loads (C), and whole-foot mesh heights (D). Right is solid blue; left is dashed orange. All 1,200 samples per series are retained. Reference lines mark the \ang{-1} guard, \qtylist{7.36;15}{\newton} load criteria, and \qty{5}{\milli\meter} clearance. Negative heights indicate soft-contact penetration. Ideal reference tensions and engineering allocation operate with \qty{77.5}{\percent} vertical assistance.} \label{fig:app_cad_supported_traces} \end{figure} Contact handover uses a smooth confidence based on measured load rather than assigning full planned support at initial contact. With $u=\operatorname{clip}[(F-F_0)/(F_1-F_0),0,1]$, confidence is $c=u^2(3-2u)$, using nominal thresholds $F_0=\qty{1}{\newton}$ and $F_1=\qty{5}{\newton}$. Phase preferences are normalized before multiplication by confidence. Requested support is filtered with a \qty{10}{\milli\second} time constant and bounded by the current contact confidence. This calculation allocates muscle targets; it does not apply additional external support. A swing-clearance task uses measured minimum foot-mesh height and RG-event-derived swing progress to request muscle-mediated lift. Its velocity feedback is part of the task controller, rather than a change in physical joint damping. The bounded allocator maps this task through hip/knee torque targets while excluding root and distal-foot coordinates. All 26 saved numerical arrays agree exactly in the fresh repeat and reference-backend replay. The retained model, route, controller, and dependency hashes agree with the local files, and current compiled geometry reproduces the recorded foot heights. A historical compiled-model binary was not retained, so these checks cannot establish its binary identity. Five of eight local perturbation cases passed; the other three reached the predictive knee margin. The nominal result therefore does not establish broad parameter robustness. Retained soft contacts still produced transient penetrations of \qtylist{16.13;12.91}{\milli\meter}. Lower-support rungs failed the retained knee guard. These latest tests supersede the earlier stance-only example as evidence of supported CAD stepping. The older matched separation-control comparison remains a distinct causal test: at unchanged \qty{80}{\percent} assistance, the added controller reduced reversed ankle ordering from \qty{67}{\percent} to zero. It is not a matched comparison with the later \qty{77.5}{\percent} configuration. Route sites can act through ideal rigid offsets outside the CAD surfaces; bracket mass, compliance, packaging, and collision-aware wrapping remain separate verification requirements.

335–364 \subsection{Pathway Deletions and Stimulus Tests}\label{sec:app_w2l_perturbations} For the sensory intervention I set nominal weight support to 0.25 and compared the intact controller with deletions at the same drive, mechanical support, and remaining fitted parameters. The evaluator applies a common \qty{40}{\newton} forward push at $8\leq t<8.15$\,s, including in the intact condition. The separately named forward-push file reproduces all of the control's saved arrays and thus supplies no unforced comparison. Each new neural input is held for \qty{2}{\milli\second}, spanning two \qty{1}{\milli\second} physics steps. The input vector has four heel/toe contact ports, twelve Ib ports, two brief initiation inputs, and four tonic RG drives. Each contact port reached \qty{5.30489}{\nano\ampere} in the intact trial. Each leg's pooled extensor-force current went to all six Ib ports on that side, with maxima of \qty{0.12237}{\nano\ampere} left and \qty{0.12263}{\nano\ampere} right. The circuit has internal Ia- and II-labeled relay connections, but this evaluator supplies neither independent per-muscle length nor velocity through an external Ia or II port. The combined sensory deletion set 35 nonzero fitted parameters to zero, including the contact and pooled-Ib input amplitudes, heel-cascade and crossed-heel connections, and Ia-, II-, and Ib-labeled projections to rhythm, pattern, and segmental pathways. All sixteen external contact and Ib input currents then remained zero. In the separate Ib-only deletion the twelve Ib inputs went to zero and the contact currents remained. The comparison tests the contribution of the specified set of sensory inputs and connections to bilateral coordination. I counted primary heel strikes over $4.5\leq t<30$\,s from rising crossings above \qty{20}{\newton}, accepting successive strikes on the same side only when they were more than \qty{0.45}{\second} apart. Median phase locates a left strike between two successive right strikes. The historical criterion required a finite trial without a fall, at least six strikes per side, phase in $[0.35,0.65]$, and forward displacement greater than \qty{0.05}{\meter}. The intact condition yielded 34 left and 22 right strikes, phase 0.498, and \qty{2.239}{\meter} progression. The combined deletion yielded 20 left and 31 right strikes and \qty{1.996}{\meter} progression, but its phase of 0.223 failed alternation. Ib-only deletion yielded 19 left and 20 right strikes, phase 0.494, and \qty{2.302}{\meter}. Fresh replays reproduced all 15 numerical trajectory arrays for each condition. Mild and strong drive reductions, reversal to a \qty{40}{\newton} backward push, and individual Ia, Ib, Renshaw, crossed-heel, and heel-cascade deletions retained the historical supported-stepping criterion. Renshaw deletion reduced forward displacement to \qty{0.081}{\meter}, and commissural deletion failed bilateral stepping. 351–419 \subsection{Incomplete Literature-Controller Transfer}\label{sec:app_dirusso_attempt} The custom SCONE runtime for the model of \citet{dirusso_investigating_2023} was subsequently built, and a \qty{10}{\second} replay in the original mechanical model reproduced the native baseline, including an exact saved fresh-process repeat. This verifies execution of that reference stack; it does not validate transfer to Gait2392 or establish physiological validity. An exploratory adaptation reproduced its corrected Python equations in a compiled implementation within $3.34\times10^{-16}$ over 2000 steps. Nevertheless, its 960-trial duration-and-speed search selected a tumbling trajectory, which was rejected as walking. Equation agreement did not establish transfer of the source controller's locomotion. The native baseline and rejected adaptation are distinct results: reproducing the original stack supplies a reference for transfer, while the unsuccessful search remains rejected as walking. \section{Spiking-Neuron Comparisons}\label{sec:spiking_methods}\label{sec:spiking_results}\label{sec:disc_spiking} The spiking variants replace afferent, rhythm-generator, and interneuron populations while retaining graded motor neurons. Synapses onto motor neurons are calibrated by mean conductance, preserving the continuous activation interface. This construction does not require the network dynamics or resulting movement to remain unchanged. Calibration and full conversion tables are in Appendix~\ref{app:neuron_equations}. Action potentials introduce voltage fluctuations absent from a graded signal, so raw membrane-voltage variability is not an equivalent measure of controller output. Figure~\ref{fig:spike_ground} compares filtered neural readouts, muscle activation, and joint motion under the same drive, support, and selected parameters. For a hardware comparison using pulse-width modulation (PWM), both signals would need to pass through the same command encoder and valve limits. \subsection{MuJoCo Network and Ground Evaluation} Under constant drive, the non-spiking and spiking rhythm periods were \qtylist{0.399;0.621}{\second}, respectively. The spiking period is \qty{55.6}{\percent} longer. Timing agreement therefore requires more than copying the selected parameters. The historical untuned spiking variant produced bilateral cycling in a supported ground evaluation where the non-spiking winner cycled predominantly on one side (Table~\ref{tab:spike_ground}; Figure~\ref{fig:spike_ground}). 423–466 \section{Execution, Provenance, and Reproduction Tools}\label{sec:app_execution_provenance} Runtime measurements retain their denominator and experimental scope. One externally timed complete-process Gait2392 comparison used the source-setting 1,025-cell implementation selected by \texttt{hunt}: native SNS versus the sparse backend in a \qty{16}{\second} stage-1 protocol, including \qty{0.6}{\second} of held-pose preparation and \qty{15.4}{\second} on the physics clock. Wall time decreased from \qty{199.624}{\second} to \qty{29.284}{\second}, a factor of 6.82, while the neural-call portion decreased from \qty{179.079}{\second} to \qty{9.780}{\second}, a factor of 18.31. All 45 captured numerical arrays and the compiled-model hash agreed. These are one paired observation, rather than repeated throughput estimates; neither ratio should be substituted for another controller or workload. The later compact backend is an incremental comparison against an already accelerated sparse implementation. Warm neural-kernel timing used the median of three 3,000-update measurements after an independent 1,200-step state comparison in each family. The 1.613 ratio belongs to the 1,033-cell Hunt-PT+RC circuit. A separate paired \qty{16}{\second} stage-4 diagnostic with that controller measured evaluator wall times of \qtylist{92.944;91.516}{\second}, giving a 1.016 ratio. All 62 saved numerical arrays and compiled-model hashes agreed exactly. The single diagnostic pair is not a repeated throughput estimate. 434–479 \section{Execution, Provenance, and Reproduction Tools}\label{sec:app_execution_provenance} I timed one complete Gait2392 process per backend for the 1,025-cell…
434–479 \section{Execution, Provenance, and Reproduction Tools}\label{sec:app_execution_provenance} I timed one complete Gait2392 process per backend for the 1,025-cell source configuration selected by \texttt{hunt}. Its \qty{16}{\second} stage-1 protocol included \qty{0.6}{\second} of held-pose preparation and \qty{15.4}{\second} on the physics clock. Elapsed process time was \qty{199.624}{\second} with native SNS and \qty{29.284}{\second} with the sparse backend, a 6.82-fold speedup. Within those processes, accumulated neural-call time was \qty{179.079}{\second} and \qty{9.780}{\second}, an 18.31-fold speedup. All 45 captured numerical arrays and the compiled-model hash agreed. I next compared compact and sparse implementations of the 1,033-cell Hunt-PT+RC circuit. After a separate 1,200-step state-agreement check in each family, the median of three 3,000-update warm neural-kernel timings was \qty{0.8953}{\second} for sparse and \qty{0.5551}{\second} for compact, a factor of 1.613. In one paired \qty{16}{\second} stage-4 diagnostic, evaluator time fell from \qty{92.944}{\second} to \qty{91.516}{\second}, a factor of 1.016. That interval included simulation, capture, and assessment, excluding imports, pre-run setup, and final serialization. All 62 numerical arrays and compiled-model hashes agreed. The backend preserves summation order. Spiking and electrical channels continue to use their existing implementations. In the bilateral-RG W2L circuit with Ib input ports, the ratio was 1.425 for one warm block of 10,000 neural updates per backend following 5,000 state-comparison steps. The paired \qty{30}{\second} body trajectories agreed exactly. This measurement times the isolated W2L neural updates. Only the first Gait2392 comparison above timed the complete process externally. Table~\ref{tab:app_script_map} gives the executable sources. Section~\ref{sec:app_knee_transient} includes a complete run from two portable model files and compares the output with the expected JSON. For the newer coupled experiments, the available records vary by test. The noise and pulse runs include the applied currents and aligned states. For BiW2Lvar+, the archived launch adapter and full consumed command stream are unavailable (Section~\ref{sec:app_biw2l_air}). The CAD repeat preserves model and dependency hashes and matched numerical arrays, while the original compiled binary is unavailable (Section~\ref{sec:app_cad_supported_stance}). EDIT-017		bbolen83 · 50-database.tex:68 (live), character 10,488, 31 Oct, 9:52 am.Empty string
\begin{samepage} For example, a researcher may ask whether type Ia feedback excites the muscle from which it originates or inhibits its antagonist. SADb contains separate…
\begin{samepage} For example, a researcher may ask whether type Ia feedback excites the muscle from which it originates or inhibits its antagonist. SADb contains separate pathway records for ``Ia monosynaptic excitation'' and ``Ia inhibitory.'' These describe complementary connections: direct excitation of the agonist motor pool and inhibition of an antagonist through an inhibitory interneuron. The researcher can inspect the linked studies and model records to compare their target muscles, intervening neurons, and task conditions before choosing how to implement the sensory afferent pathways. The afferent class alone does not specify the sign of every connection.\par \end{samepage}

EDIT-021		bbolen83 · 50-database.tex:102 (live), character 16,547, 31 Oct, 9:52 am.
Robotic tests can also return predictions to biology. \citet{ijspeert_swimming_2007} implemented a spinal-cord model in a salamander robot that switched between walking a…
Robotic tests can also return predictions to biology. \citet{ijspeert_swimming_2007} implemented a spinal-cord model in a salamander robot that switched between walking and swimming as control drive changed. The model predicted lower intrinsic frequencies for limb oscillators than for body oscillators, and the authors presented biological data supporting that prediction. This exchange illustrates the broader motivation for the humanoid project: biological evidence informs a controllable model, whose behavior can generate questions for subsequent biological experiments.

EDIT-305		bbolen83 · 94-AppendixC.tex:477 (live), character 46,563, 31 Oct, 9:53 am.Empty string
For each site, domain coverage is estimated from the saved grid by \begin{equation} C=100\,\frac{\sum_{i,j} w_iw_j D_{ij}M_{ij}}{\sum_{i,j}w_iw_jD_{ij}}, \label{eq:angul…
For each site, domain coverage is estimated from the saved grid by \begin{equation} C=100\,\frac{\sum_{i,j} w_iw_j D_{ij}M_{ij}}{\sum_{i,j}w_iw_jD_{ij}}, \label{eq:angular_domain_coverage} \end{equation} where $D_{ij}$ indicates inclusion in the evaluated domain and $M_{ij}$ indicates that the human torque requirement is met. On each uniformly spaced axis, trapezoidal weights are one-half at the outer endpoints and one at interior samples; the common angular increments cancel in the ratio. Active-force coverage uses the positive-tension indicator in place of $M_{ij}$. For single-DoF routes, this becomes interval coverage; the repeated fixed coordinate cancels. These are grid-based quadrature estimates, rather than exact continuous-domain boundaries. Each site's fraction is normalized before the equal-site mean is calculated.
Author's recorded insert shown in the live Review panel. The source reconciliation used the visible proposed text.Reconciled with local revisions; any live acceptance or rejection belongs to Ben.

Eleven targetable rhythm and gait metrics passed their acceptance bands. The bilateral contact-driven model's rhythm generators had periods of \qty{0.450}{} and \qty{0.467}{\second}; the modern W2L model showed joint oscillations at \qty{2.25}{\Hz}. The Li-model rerun reached \qty{0.637}{\meter\per\second} with step periods near \qty{1.3}{\second} \citep{li_bipedal_2017}. These are reference-model checks, not results for the combined Deng--Li model or evidence of unsupported walking. A separate MuJoCo translation of the Li controller and body did not reproduce that original-simulator walking result. In a 20-second ground test, mean forward speed was \qty{0.040}{\meter\per\second}, with eleven detected right-foot stance episodes and none on the left. The right-side period of \qty{1.294}{\second} alone therefore did not establish bilateral stepping. This failure distinguishes reproduction of the source model from successful transfer to another simulator. \section{Tuning and Supported Evaluations}\label{sec:sns_tuning_methods}\label{sec:sns_curriculum} Free network gains were searched with a tree-structured Parzen estimator, with each candidate evaluated through simulation. The staged procedure separates suspended rhythm generation, sensory feedback, standing, and ground evaluations (Table~\ref{tab:sns_curriculum}). Finalists were checked under \qty{1}{\percent} multiplicative parameter perturbations and changes in drive. Such checks probe the neighborhood of a selected configuration; they do not establish robustness to untested loads or removal of the harness. The cycle-shape metric assigns zero to perfect agreement. A run without detectable cycles returns $-25$ as a reserved failure value. Because this value is numerically greater than the genuine-gait scores below, failed runs must be identified before ranking scores. That value itself is not a valid measure of gait quality. The curriculum is shown in Table~\ref{tab:sns_curriculum} in Chapter~\ref{ch:sns}. \subsection{Reference-Library Comparison}\label{sec:gait_library_results} The 43-cycle library contains 32 running trials from eight subjects at four speeds, ten walking references, and a predicted-walking cycle. The walking references span prescribed speeds of \qtyrange{0.5}{2.0}{\meter\per\second} and self-selected trials. The production network (\texttt{s3k}), reduced-connection network (\texttt{s3kpruned}), six-group activation network (\texttt{syn6}), and W2L-organization network (\texttt{w2lvar}) were scored without treating a good tuning-reference score as evidence of generalization. Library scores were substantially more negative (Table~\ref{tab:gait_sweep}), and the evaluations showed unilateral or asymmetric gait. This comparison bounds transfer to the available references; it does not validate normal human walking. Table~\ref{tab:gait_sweep} uses the production pipeline's cycle-shape kinematic score. The tuning-reference column reports each configuration on its tuning reference; the library mean averages that score over all 43 reference cycles. \begin{table}[htbp] \caption{Four controller configurations evaluated on their tuning reference and the 43-cycle reference library. For genuine gait cycles, larger kinematic scores indicate closer agreement; zero is perfect agreement.} \label{tab:gait_sweep} \begin{tabular}{@{}lrrl@{}} \toprule & \textbf{Tuning-ref.} & \textbf{Library mean} & \textbf{Most negative reference} \\ & \textbf{score} & \textbf{score (43 refs)} & \textbf{(score)} \\ \midrule \texttt{s3k} & $-186.4$ & $-8776$ & \texttt{Case\_40\_motion} ($-8841$) \\ \texttt{s3kpruned} & $-176.1$ & $-6924$ & \texttt{Case\_40\_motion} ($-6986$) \\ \texttt{syn6} & $-200.4$ & $-9961$ & \texttt{Case\_40\_motion} ($-10040$) \\ \texttt{w2lvar} & $-144.5$ & $-5808$ & \texttt{subject08\_Run\_20002} ($-5896$) \\ \bottomrule \end{tabular} \end{table} \subsection{Direct Kinematics Overlay}\label{sec:app_ik_overlay} The saved human-model controller reproduced its cycle-shape score of $-145.07$ in an independent process. The direct overlay in Figure~\ref{fig:gait2392_ik_overlay} uses the Gait2392 walking inverse-kinematics trajectory and its paired ground-reaction-force file. Model cycles begin when vertical foot loading crosses \qty{50}{\newton}; reference stance is defined using the same force threshold. Ten cycles per side, with durations between 0.35 and 2.5 seconds, were retained using the existing cycle detector. Each leg was aligned to its own onset and resampled over the gait cycle, without fitting phase, subtracting the mean angle, or scaling amplitude. Mean cycle periods were \qty{1.285}{\second} on the right and \qty{1.254}{\second} on the left, compared with \qty{1.233}{\second} and \qty{1.213}{\second} in the reference. Model stance fractions were 0.710 and 0.649, compared with 0.610 in the reference. Table~\ref{tab:ik_absolute_rmse} reports absolute-angle errors between mean cycles. These errors retain posture offsets and are distinct from the mean-removed cycle-shape errors used in tuning. The hip and knee mismatches show why cycle counts and a scalar score must be accompanied by the trajectories and contact timing. \begin{table}[htbp] \centering \caption[Absolute-angle errors against Gait2392 inverse kinematics]{Unshifted absolute-angle RMSE between mean model and reference cycles, in degrees.} \label{tab:ik_absolute_rmse} \begin{tabular}{lrr} \toprule Joint & Right & Left \\ \midrule Hip & 28.03 & 24.65 \\ Knee & 27.44 & 28.45 \\ Ankle & 8.71 & 7.38 \\ \bottomrule \end{tabular} \end{table} \subsection{Pathway-Removal Tests}\label{sec:walker_variant_results} Individual pathway classes were removed from each selected configuration and the model re-evaluated on its tuning reference (Table~\ref{tab:walker_ablations}). Removing Ia feedback from the production configuration eliminated detected cycles. Other removals changed the score without necessarily eliminating movement. The different response of the six-synergy variant shows that these dependencies belong to the tested network configurations, rather than constituting a general claim about biological necessity. The systematic pattern-formation and motoneuron stimulation series for these full Gait2392 networks was not completed. The reduced-circuit tests in Section~\ref{sec:app_layer_stimulus} address a separate, smaller model. \begin{table}[htbp] \caption{Pathway-removal tests on the tuning reference. Full-model rows give unchanged baselines. A score of $-25$ is the fixed no-cycle fallback and denotes failure, despite exceeding the genuine-gait scores.} \label{tab:walker_ablations} \small\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.20\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.24\textwidth}@{}} \toprule \textbf{Configuration} & \textbf{Removed pathway} & \textbf{Score} \\ \midrule \texttt{s3k} (full model) & none & $-186.4$ \\ & Ia afferents & $-25$ (no cycles detected) \\ & all afferent classes & $-267.8$ \\ & Renshaw cells & $-267.3$ \\ & group-II afferents & $-255.2$ \\ & Ib afferents & $-180.3$ \\ & contact channels & $-183.9$ \\ & interleg commissural pathways & $-174.9$ \\ \addlinespace \texttt{syn6} (full model) & none & $-200.4$ \\ & all afferent classes & $-190.5$ \\ \addlinespace \texttt{w2lvar} (full model) & none & $-144.5$ \\ & Ia afferents & $-273.5$ \\ & weak rhythm-generator mutual excitation & $-279.9$ \\ & Renshaw cells & $-142.4$ \\ \bottomrule \end{tabularx} \end{table} \section{Spiking-Neuron Comparisons}\label{sec:spiking_methods}\label{sec:spiking_results}\label{sec:disc_spiking} The spiking variants replace afferent, rhythm-generator, and interneuron populations while retaining graded motor neurons. Synapses onto motor neurons are calibrated by mean conductance, preserving the continuous activation interface. This construction does not require the network dynamics or resulting movement to remain unchanged. Calibration and full conversion tables are in Appendix~\ref{app:neuron_equations}. \subsection{MuJoCo Network and Ground Evaluation} Under constant drive, the non-spiking and spiking rhythm periods were \qty{0.399}{} and \qty{0.621}{\second}, respectively (Figure~\ref{fig:spike_rhythm}). The spiking period is \qty{55.6}{\percent} longer. Timing agreement therefore requires more than copying the selected parameters. The untuned spiking variant produced bilateral cycling in a ground evaluation where the non-spiking winner cycled predominantly on one side (Table~\ref{tab:spike_ground}; Figure~\ref{fig:spike_ground}). This individual comparison motivates further controlled testing; it does not establish superiority of spiking control. \begin{figure}[htbp] \centering \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{spiking_mirror_rhythm.pdf} % ALT TEXT: see spiking_mirror_rhythm_alt.txt beside the figure file. \caption[Constant-drive rhythm, non-spiking versus spiking]{Constant-drive rhythm in the non-spiking network (A) and spiking variant (B). The observed periods are \qty{0.399}{} and \qty{0.621}{\second}; the latter is \qty{55.6}{\percent} longer. The panels use different neural readouts: graded population difference versus individual spiking cells.} \label{fig:spike_rhythm} \end{figure} \begin{table}[htbp] \caption{Supported ground evaluation at the same selected parameters (16 s), with no spiking re-tuning. Both use the supported evaluation protocol.} \label{fig:spike_ground} \end{figure} \subsection{Simulink Demonstrations} The graded and spiking knee-reflex demonstrations were compared over matched \qty{5}{\second} runs (Table~\ref{tab:spike_knee}; Figure~\ref{fig:spike_knee}). The beverage example retained approximately the same sag, \ang{2.02} versus \ang{2.00}, compared with \ang{7.85} without the reflex. These examples demonstrate transfer of particular reflex responses after calibration, not invariance of all controlled behavior to neural coding. \begin{table}[htbp] \caption{Knee-reflex demonstration, graded baseline versus spiking twin at the matched operating point (5 s runs; settle statistics over the last \qty{2}{\second}).} \label{tab:spike_knee} \small\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.42\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X@{}} \toprule \textbf{Metric} & \textbf{Baseline} & \textbf{Spiking twin} \\ \midrule Knee angle at \qty{0.05}{\second} & \ang{15.79} & \ang{15.79} \\ Settle mean (last \qty{2}{\second}) & \ang{43.5} & \ang{44.4} \\ Settle range & \ang{41.6}--\ang{44.9} & \ang{41.9}--\ang{46.7} \\ Extensor / flexor activation (window means) & 0.53 / 0.53 & 0.58 / 0.56 \\ Antagonist alternation & \qty{0.5}{\hertz}--\qty{2}{\hertz} & \qty{7.4}{\hertz} (dominant peak) \\ Ia interneuron firing at settle & graded, subthreshold & \qty{95}{\hertz}; Ib \qty{0}{\hertz} \\ \bottomrule \end{tabularx} \end{table} \begin{figure}[htbp] \centering \includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{simulink_knee_spiking_twin.pdf} % ALT TEXT: see simulink_knee_spiking_twin_alt.txt beside the figure file. \caption[Simulink knee-reflex twin, baseline versus spiking]{Knee-reflex demonstration at the matched operating point: graded baseline (solid indigo) and spiking variant (dashed orange). Full \qty{5}{\second} run (A) and final \qty{2}{\second} window (B). The spiking response has a slightly wider oscillation band.} \label{fig:spike_knee} \end{figure} \subsection{AnimatLab Conversions} The tested spiking conversions did not preserve the graded models' rhythm (Table~\ref{tab:spike_animatlab}). Converting thresholds while retaining graded synapses preserved near-baseline oscillations, whereas converting synaptic amplitudes and decay changed or eliminated alternation. These results identify the synapse conversion as a limitation of this mapping. They do not demonstrate that a suitably calibrated spiking implementation cannot reproduce the reference behavior. \begin{table}[htbp] \caption{AnimatLab spiking conversions versus their graded baselines (5.1 s headless runs; joint ranges in degrees where the rhythm survives). The thresholds-only ablation converts the neurons but keeps the graded synapses.} \label{tab:spike_animatlab} \small \begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}} \toprule \textbf{Variant} & \textbf{Baseline (graded)} & \textbf{Spiking copy} \\ \midrule Two-layer walker & \qty{0.443}{\second} rhythm; & dead (2 spikes; \\ & hip $-15$ to $+23$, knee $-1$ to $+60$ & hip pinned near $+6$) \\ Bilateral variant & \qty{0.462}{\second}; antiphase $-0.42$ cycle & latched; one side at \qty{442}{\hertz}--\qty{450}{\hertz} \\ Reorganized project & latched (tonic drives) & synchronous \qty{11.8}{\hertz} co-bursting; \\ (standalone export) & & same-leg envelopes $r = 1.000$ at lag 0 \\ Thresholds only & \multirow{2}{*The BPA-actuated walker exercised the chain from neural activation through the actuator force model to multi-joint motion. In the supported air-stepping run, the virtual harness carried \qty{70}{\percent} of the \qty{411}{\newton} body weight. The period was \qty{1.542}{\second}, knee RoM was \ang{63.5}, and peak forces were \qty{880}{} and \qty{1372}{\newton}, at a peak pressure of \qty{372}{\kPa}. With split left/right drives and retuned feedback, the W2L robot progressed \qty{1.72}{\meter} over \qty{30}{\second} under the support protocol. It retained progression at \qty{60}{\percent} drive and passed 12 of 12 perturbed reruns, but did not satisfy the strict contact-duty walking criterion; below that drive the gait ceased. The mechanical support was not weaned. In the separate human-model drive-weaning study, the selected score improved from $-161.57$ to $-145.07$, with ten contact cycles per leg and contact duties of 0.71 and 0.65. It passed eight of eight perturbed reruns and retained bilateral stepping down to zero handus, repeatable supported stepping was demonstrated, while harness-free walking was notfive seconds, knee excursions were \ang{1.35} right and \ang{0.95} left, while the vertical rig carried \qty{94.68}{\percent} of body weight and angular stabilization remained active. Disabling muscle forces under the same support settings caused lumbar failure after \qty{0.202}{\second}. This comparison establishes a contribution of the muscle controller under strong assistance, not independent balance. A separate run with rhythmic drive completed its horizon, but alternating gait and progression after settling have not been verified. A subsequent matched 40-second comparison retained the same geometry, initial pose, reference-tension capacities, and external assistance in both conditions. During 15--40 s, vertical assistance supplied 80\% of body weight. The additional control combines muscle-mediated separation feedback (160 N/m, capped at 20 N) with a 6\% activation floor on currently abducting routes, compared with the 5\% global floor (Table~\ref{tab:cad_abductor}). Full recorded trajectories reproduce bit for bit between fresh reference and accelerated runs. This is numerical replay within the specified model, rather than a robustness test. \begin{table}[htbp] \centering\small \caption[Matched CAD-model separation-control comparison]{Matched CAD-model comparison over 15--40 s. Vertical assistance remains 80\% of body weight in both conditions.} \label{tab:cad_abductor} \begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}Xrr@{}} \toprule Measure & Control off & Control on \\ \midrule Reversed ankle ordering (\%) & 67 & 0 \\ Minimum ankle separation (mm) & $-38.3$ & 142.4 \\ Median ankle separation (mm) & $-6.2$ & 159.0 \\ Mean right TFL activation (\%) & 22.3 & 81.8 \\ Mean left TFL activation (\%) & 99.6 & 99.8 \\ Maximum sampled knee-loop error (mm) & 4.485 & 0.387 \\ Maximum sampled interleg penetration (mm) & 2.700 & 0 \\ \bottomrule \end{tabularx} \end{table} The 262-neuron controller receives 230 inputs and drives 54 reference-tension actuators. Its MN target-current compensation cancels incoming PF and reflex synaptic currents to track the force allocator's targets. Consequently, the effective comparison concerns RG timing, engineered force allocation, and mechanical response, rather than normal PF/reflex authority over activation. Toe-out and small post-ramp knee excursions remain, and the stepping acceptance tests fail. The foot attachment is an unverified assembly datum. A separate hypothesis translates each whole foot 60 mm toward the proximal shank, retaining tibial geometry and mass. With otherwise unchanged hybrid control, median ankle separation changes from 159.0 to \qty{57.3}{\milli\meter}, and sampled interleg penetration reaches \qty{1.316}{\milli\meter}. A separate W2L-organization timing probe has 544 neurons and 230 inputs; four named ankle indices alias knee-PF cells and are not additional neurons. Its shifted-foot condition passes bilateral unloading and lifting counts but fails repeated alternating foot apices and forward progression. The inherited MN compensation remains active, so this is not a full PF-driven controller comparison. Route sites can lie outside CAD surfaces and transmit force through their named parent bodies, equivalent to ideal rigid offset brackets or guides. Bracket mass, compliance, packaging, and collision-aware wrapping remain unmodeled. These attachment assumptions are separate from verification of the source moving-point functions and complete path derivatives. The older 20-secondN) & Restored scale (N
